Title: Origenis in Epistolam ad Romanos Commentariorum libri (Rufinus's Latin), Origenis Opera Omnia, Tomus VI: Books I to V of the Commentary on the Epistle to the Romans
Creator: Origen (Rufinus of Aquileia, translator and abridger, c. 406-410); edited by Carolus and Carol. Vincent. De la Rue (Maurists), newly revised by Carol. Henric. Eduard. Lommatzsch
Publisher: Berolini (Berlin): Sumtibus Haude et Spener, 1836
Publication Date: 1836
Rights: Public Domain
Language: lat (Rufinus's Latin; Lommatzsch's notes and variant readings are Latin, with some Greek)
Source: Internet Archive item origenous-ta-heuriskomena-panta-t.-6-1836; the item's own OCR text file (djvu.txt), fetched 2026-10-26 by the Library thread.
Provenance note: The item's metadata names the work, the 1836 Berlin imprint and a Public Domain Mark 1.0 licence (licenseurl creativecommons.org/publicdomain/mark/1.0/); it was published in 1836, so it is out of US copyright by date as well. The text below is the host's OCR, unchanged. Only this header was added. The title page was read in the OCR and names the volume and the editors above.
Content note: Books I-V, preceded by the prefaces. Books VI-X are volume VII. The Latin is Rufinus's translation. In his preface (npnf203) he says the Greek commentary ran to fifteen volumes and that he was asked to abbreviate it; this Latin has ten books. Jerome accused him of altering Origen (that quarrel is also in npnf203). It is evidence for Origen as Rufinus gave him, not Origen verbatim. The OCR is readable Latin with occasional misreadings and stray superscript marks from the footnote apparatus. Check any quotation against the page image before it goes into a record.

tes, vertici ejus imposuerunt spineam coronam. Quis!) 
autem honor est, qui his omnibus virtutibus ab homine 
debetur, in Evangeliis edocemur, dicente Domino: »ut ?) 
omnes .honorificent Filium, sicut honorificant Patrem.

et studium defert sapientiae et Justitiae et veritati, omni- 
busque his, quae esse dicuntur Christus.?) . Hoc ergo 
modo quaerunt gloriam et honorem et incorruptionem, 
qui festinant ad aeternam vitam. Sed et incorruptio quid 
indicet, cito sciemus, si quod' ei contrarium est discutia- 
mus. Incorruptio contrariam habet corruptionem. Cor- 
ruptio duobus modis accidere dicitur: corporaliter et 
spiritualiter. Corporaliter accidit, cum evenerit illud, quod 
scriptum est: ,si^) quis templum Dei corruperit, cor-
